\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon})=0\,.

We are now in position to prove Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Fix
1\leq p<\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
. By Lemma
7.6
, and in view of
the definition (
2.26
) of
{\mathscr{J}}^{(p)}(\cdot)
, and formula
(
5
) for
{\mathbb{I}}^{(p)}(\cdot)
, to complete the proof
of Proposition
7.1
it remains to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{D}}_{r})\;{\mathbb{I}}^{(p)}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}}(\omega(\;\cdot\;|\;{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}))\;+\;\sum_{r=1}^{o_{p}}\sum_{s\neq r}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{r}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{s}}\omega({\mathcal{M}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime})\,.

(7.20)

Split the second sum distinguishing transient states belonging to an
equivalent class with two or more elements from the singletons to
rewrite it as

\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}\setminus{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}}\omega({\mathcal{M}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime})\,+\,\sum_{r=1}^{k_{p}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime})\,.

We turn to the proof of (
7.20
). By convexity and the definition
of
\mu_{\epsilon}
,

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{D}}_{r})\,\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{r},\epsilon})\,+\,\sum_{r=1}^{k_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{C}}_{r})\,\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r},\epsilon})\,.

By item (1) in Lemma
7.7
and the fact that

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)
jumps to nearest neighbors only,

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{D}}_{r})\,\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{r},\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{D}}_{r})\,{\mathbb{I}}^{(p)}_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}}(\omega(\,\cdot\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}))\,+\,\sum_{r=1}^{l_{p}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}\setminus{\mathfrak{D}}_{r}^{p}}\,\omega({\mathcal{M}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime})\,.

By items (2) and (3) of Lemma
7.7
,

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{k_{p}}\omega({\mathfrak{C}}_{r})\,\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{C}}_{r},\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{r=1}^{k_{p}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{r}}\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{p}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime})\,.

To complete the proof the proposition, it remains to recollect the
previous estimates.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.7

The proof requires some preliminary estimates. We only focus on item
(1) since a similar argument applies to items (2) and (3). Fix an
equivalent class
{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
having two or more
elements. As in Section
4
, we slightly perturb

\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
in order to apply Lemma
3.2
to estimate

{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon})
.

Given
\delta>0
, let
\mu^{\delta}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
be
the probability measure given by the density

m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}(x)\,:=\,\frac{m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)+\delta}{1+\delta},

where
m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
has been introduced in
(
7.18
). The proof of Assertion

4.7
yields the next result.

Assertion 7.8
.

For each
\epsilon>0
,

\lim_{\delta\to 0}\Big|\;{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta})\;-\;\frac{1}{4\epsilon}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}{\textsf{a}}\,\Big\{\epsilon\Big[\frac{(m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta})^{\prime}}{m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}}\,+\,\frac{{\textsf{a}}^{\prime}}{{\textsf{a}}}\Big]\,-\,\frac{{\textsf{b}}}{{\textsf{a}}}\Big\}^{2}d\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}\;\Big|\;=\,0\,.

Next results follows from this estimate and the definition of the
test function
J_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
.

Assertion 7.9
.

For each
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{k=l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{\,r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}\int_{B_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,r}}^{p}\cup\,\Lambda_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,r}}^{p}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;\frac{e^{-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)/\epsilon}}{c_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\epsilon)}\;dx\,.

Proof.

Since
\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}
converges weakly to

\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
, by the lower-semicontinuity of

{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
,

{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon})\,\leq\,\liminf_{\delta\to 0}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}).

At this point, recall Assertion
7.8
.
Differentiate
m_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\delta}
, expand the square and
apply the dominated convergence theorem, to get that

{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon})\,\leq\,\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;\frac{e^{-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)/\epsilon}}{c_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\epsilon)}\;dx\,.

To complete the proof, recall that
J_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
vanishes on

{\mathbb{T}}\setminus\Omega_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
and that, by
(
7.16
),
J_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\prime}
vanishes on the
intervals
C^{r,r+1}_{k-1,k}
.
∎

The next results states that in Assertion
7.9
the
integrals over the binding sets are negligible.

Assertion 7.10
.

For each
l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\leq k\leq r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1
,
1\leq r\leq{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}
,

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,\epsilon}{c_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\epsilon)}\,\int_{B^{r}_{k-1,k}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;e^{-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)/\epsilon}\;dx\,=\,0\,.

Proof.

By (
2.18
), (
2.19
) and (
A.6
)

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,\leq\,e^{\,[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}))]\,/\,\epsilon}\,.

(7.21)

Mind that this inequality may be strict if the local maximum

\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k}
is the left or rightmost, that is, if it is equal
to
\sigma^{-}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
or
\sigma^{+}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
.

By Assertion
7.5
, for each fixed
\epsilon>0
, the
expression appearing in the statement of the lemma is bounded above by

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\sqrt{\epsilon}\,\int_{B^{r}_{k-1,k}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;e^{\,[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)]\,/\,\epsilon}\;dx\,.

Here and below,
C_{0}
represents a finite constant independent of

\epsilon
, whose value may change from line to line. By the
definition of
J_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(\cdot)
over the binding sets

B^{r}_{k-1,k}
, given below (
7.17
), this expression is
equal to

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\sqrt{\epsilon}\,\int_{B^{r}_{k-1,k}}\big[\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;e^{\,[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)]\,/\,\epsilon}\;dx\,.

By a Taylor expansion of
S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\cdot)
around
\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k}
,
and by definition of
\delta^{r}_{k-1,k}
,
\eta^{r}_{k-1,k}
, on

B^{r}_{k-1,k}
,

\displaystyle\exp\big\{\,[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)]\,/\,\epsilon\,\big\}\,

\displaystyle=\,[1+o(\epsilon)]\,\exp\big\{\,-\,S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})\,(\delta^{r}_{k-1,k}+\eta^{r}_{k-1,k})^{2}/2\epsilon\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\,C_{0}\,\exp\big\{\,-\,S^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})\,(\delta^{r}_{k-1,k})^{2}/2\epsilon\big\}\,.

By definition of
\delta^{r}_{k-1,k}
, the last line is equal to

C_{0}\,\epsilon^{-1}
. Thus, by the bound on the derivative of

\Phi_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}
, and since the length of the interval

B^{r,\pm}_{k-1,k}
is
\eta^{r}_{k-1,k}
, the penultimate displayed
equation is bounded above by
C_{0}\,\sqrt{\epsilon}\;\eta^{r}_{k-1,k}
,
which vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
.
∎

The next Assertion completes the proof of Proposition

7.1
. It requires a simple observation. As

{\mathfrak{D}}
is a
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)
-equivalence class, the jump
rates from
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}})
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}})
to

{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1)
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1)
, respectively, may be
positive or vanish. By (
2.22
),

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))>0
if

S(\sigma_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r})\,-\,S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,=\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}

(7.22)

for some (an thus for all)
1\leq r\leq{\mathfrak{r}}_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}
. It vanishes if

S(\sigma_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r})\,-\,S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,>\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
. A similar observation holds for the jump rate

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))
.

Assertion 7.11
.

For each
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)-
equivalence class
{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{p}
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}\,\epsilon\,\sum_{k=l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}\int_{\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}}\big[\,J_{{\mathfrak{D}},\epsilon}^{\prime}(x)\,\big]^{2}(x)\;\frac{e^{-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)/\epsilon}}{c_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\epsilon)}\;dx\,\leq\,{\mathbb{I}}^{(p)}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\omega(\,\cdot\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}}))

\displaystyle\quad\,+\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}})\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))\,+\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}})\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{r}}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))\,.

Proof.

Fix
l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}\leq k\leq r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1
and

1\leq r\leq{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k-1}
. By (
7.14
),
(
7.10
), and (
7.5
), the integral
appearing in the statement of the assertion is equal to

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathbb{W}}_{k-1,k}^{r}(\epsilon)}\,:=\,K_{k-1,k}^{r}\,\sqrt{\frac{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}{2\pi}}\,\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}}{c_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\epsilon)}\,\int_{\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}}e^{S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r}-x)^{2}/\epsilon}\,e^{-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)/\epsilon}\,dx\,,

(7.23)

where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}K_{k-1,k}^{r}}\,:=\,\sqrt{\frac{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}{2\pi}}\,\big[\,{\textsf{w}}_{k-1,k}^{r,r+1}-{\textsf{w}}_{k-1,k}^{r-1,r}\,\big]^{2}\,\big[\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,-\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big]^{2}\,.

We consider two cases.

Case 1:
Assume that
k=l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
and that

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))=0
. Under this
assumption, by (
7.22
),

S(\sigma_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r})\,-\,S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}))>{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
. Hence, by Assertion
7.5
and by the definition of

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(p)}
, (
7.23
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle C_{0}\,e^{[{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}-S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})+S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}))]/\epsilon}\,\sqrt{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}\int_{\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}}\,e^{S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r}-x)^{2}/\epsilon}\,e^{[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})\,-\,S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)]/\epsilon}\,dx\,.

for some finite constant
C_{0}
independent of
\epsilon
and whose
value may change from line to line. We prove below that this
expression without the first exponential converges. Therefore, as

S(\sigma_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r})\,-\,S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}))>{\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
this expression with the first exponential vanishes in the
limit. Thus, we proved that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}}{\mathbb{W}}_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r}(\epsilon)\,=\,0\,.

Since
R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))=0
, we may
rewrite this identity in the following form

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}}{\mathbb{W}}_{l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1,l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}}^{r}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle\quad=\;-\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\big]

The same argument applies to the case
k=r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1
with

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))=0
.

Case 2:
Assume that
k=l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
and

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))>0
, or
l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}<k\leq r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
, or
k=r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1
and

R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))>0
.

By Assertion
7.5
and (
7.21
), the expression in
(
7.23
) is equal to

\displaystyle[\,1+o(\epsilon)\,]\,\frac{K_{k-1,k}^{r}}{\sum_{k\in D}\pi_{p}(k)}\,\sqrt{\frac{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}{2\pi\epsilon}}\int_{\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}}\,e^{S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r}-x)^{2}/\epsilon}\,e^{[S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})\,-\,S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)]/\epsilon}\,dx\,.

A Taylor expansion of
S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
around
\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k}
yields that

\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,\big[\,S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})\,-\,S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(x)\,\big]\,=\,-\,\frac{1}{2\epsilon}\,S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k})\,(\sigma^{r}_{k-1,k}-x)^{2}\,+\,o_{\epsilon}(1)\;,

where the remainder is uniform on
x\in\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}
. Moreover, by the definition of

\delta_{k-1,k}^{r}
, introduced in (
7.6
), and a change of
variables,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sqrt{\frac{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}{2\pi\epsilon}}\int_{\Lambda_{k-1,k}^{r}}\,e^{S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r}-x)^{2}/2\epsilon}\,\,dx\,=\,1\,.

This proves that (
7.23
) converges to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{k\in D}\pi_{p}(k)}\,\sqrt{\frac{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}{2\pi}}\,\big[\,{\textsf{w}}_{k-1,k}^{r,r+1}-{\textsf{w}}_{k-1,k}^{r-1,r}\,\big]^{2}\,\big[\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,-\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\,\frac{1}{\sum_{k\in D}\pi_{p}(k)}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\frac{1/\sqrt{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}}{\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}1/\sqrt{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{l})}\Big)^{2}}\,\big[\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,-\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big]^{2}\,.

where we used the definition (
7.7
) of
{\textsf{w}}_{k-1,k}^{r,r+1}
in
the last step.

Recall from (
2.21
) the definition of
\sigma_{p}(j,j+1)
. It follows
from the just proved convergence of the expression in (
7.23
) and
from the identity

\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\frac{1/\sqrt{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{r})}}{\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}1/\sqrt{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{l})}\Big)^{2}}\,=\,\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}(k-1,k)}

that in Case 2,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}{\mathbb{W}}_{k-1,k}^{r}(\epsilon)\,=\,\frac{1}{\sum_{l\in D}\pi_{p}(l)}\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}(k-1,k)}\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\big]^{2}

Expanding the square and since
\sigma_{p}(k,k-1)=\sigma_{p}(k-1,k)
. the previous expression is equal to

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{\sum_{l\in D}\pi_{p}(l)}\,\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}(k-1,k)}\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big[\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big]

\displaystyle\quad-\,\frac{1}{\sum_{l\in D}\pi_{p}(l)}\,\frac{1}{\sigma_{p}(k,k-1)}\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big[\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big]\,.

Multiply and divide the first line by
\pi_{p}(k-1)
. By the definition
of the function
f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
, the jump-rates
R_{p}
and the stationary
state
\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{p}
introduced in (
7.8
), (
2.22
),
and Lemma
B.1
, respectively, the first term of the
right-hand side of the previous equation is equal to

-\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\big]\,.

Similarly, the second line is equal to

-\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\big]\,.

Hence, in Case 2,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}{\mathbb{W}}_{k-1,k}^{r}(\epsilon)\,

\displaystyle\quad=\,-\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\big]

\displaystyle\quad-\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))\,\big[f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k-1))-f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\big]\,.

Note that in the case
k=l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
, the first line vanishes because

f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))=0
. Hence we obtained the same
formula for the limit in both cases. We may thus proceed using only
the formula obtained in Case 2.

Conclusion:
Recall from (
5.1
) the
definition of the reflected generator
\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}
.
Since

f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1)=f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1)=0
,
it follows from the computation performed in Case 2 that the
expression which appears on the left-hand side of the assertion is
equal to

\displaystyle-\,\sum_{k\in D}\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}f_{{\mathfrak{D}}})({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))

\displaystyle\quad+\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))^{2}\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))

\displaystyle\quad+\,\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))\,f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}))^{2}\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))\,.

By definition of
f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\cdot)
, the last two lines are equal to

\displaystyle\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}})\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l_{{\mathfrak{D}}}-1))\,+\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}})\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}})\,R_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r_{{\mathfrak{D}}}+1))\,,

and the first line can be rewritten as

\displaystyle-\,\sum_{k\in D}\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k)\,|\,{\mathfrak{D}})\,\frac{1}{f_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))}\,(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}f_{{\mathfrak{D}}})({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\,.

To complete the proof of the assertion, it remains to recall the
formula (
5.4
) for

{\mathbb{I}}^{(p)}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}(\omega(\cdot|{\mathfrak{D}}))

∎

8.
The
\Gamma-\limsup
in the last time-scale: The reversible
case

In this section, we establish the
\Gamma-\limsup
for the last
time-scale
\theta^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{\epsilon}
under the following hypothesis

Hypothesis (Rev).

Assume that one of the three conditions is fulfilled

(a)

The Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

is reducible.

(b)

The Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has one closed
irreducible class.

(c)

The Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

is irreducible, and the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

has infinitely many closed irreducible classes which are all
equivalent.

Before we proceed, we state some remarks on the cases (a)-(c). Recall
that, above the statement of Postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{10}
in Appendix

A
, we denote by
{\mathfrak{n}}_{p}
,
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
, the number
of closed irreducible classes of the lifted chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}
, up to
(
A.3
)-equivalence. By definition of
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
,
in all situations
{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\leq 1
.

Condition (a):
Under condition (a),
S(1)

may be strictly smaller than
S(0)
. Furthermore,

{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
, and the Markov chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
restricted to the unique
closed irreducible class is reversible.

Condition (b):
Under condition (b), by
postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}
all local sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
,

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, are at the same depth. Thus, by periodicity,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}))=S(1+{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))
, so that

S(1)=S(0)
. On the other hand, by definition,

{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=0
. Finally, the Markov chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is irreducible and
reversible.

Condition (c):
Under condition (c),
S(1)

may be strictly smaller than
S(0)
,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
, and
the Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is
reversible.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume that condition
(Rev)
is fulfilled. Then, the functional

{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
is a
\Gamma-\limsup
of

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}
.

Fix a measure
\mu\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
. If

{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)=+\infty
, then the bound

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,{\mathscr{I}}(\mu_{\epsilon})\leq{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)

holds trivially for any sequence that converges weakly to
\mu
. Thus,
we may assume that
{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)<+\infty
. By the
definition of
{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
, this entails that the
measure
\mu
is given by

\mu(\cdot)=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)\,,

for some fixed probability measure

\omega\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
. By Lemma B5 in
[
20
]
,
we can assume that
\omega({\mathcal{M}})>0
for every

{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.

Proof of Proposition
8.1
under the
conditions (a) and (b).

If the Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is reducible,
that is, condition (a) is in force, then each

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-equivalent class is surrounded on the left
and on the right by another
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-equivalent
class. We may thus repeat the proof of Proposition

7.1
without modifications, constructing a
recovery sequence associated to each

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-equivalent class, to prove the

\Gamma-\limsup
.

Suppose condition (b) holds. As the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is irreducible,
S(1)=S(0)
so that
B=0
. In
particular,
S(\cdot)
is periodic and well defined as a function on
the torus
{\mathbb{T}}
. In this case also the proof of Proposition

7.1
applies, this time with minor
modifications.

Fix a probability measure
\omega\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})

such that
\omega({\mathcal{M}})>0
for every

{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. The construction of the test
function follows the one presented in the previous section starting at
equation (
7.2
). Here
{\mathfrak{D}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
so that
l_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}=0
,

r_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}={\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1
.

Denote by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}f_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}}\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
the function defined in (
7.8
) (with
{\mathfrak{D}}

replaced by
{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
). Let

F_{{\mathscr{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\colon{\mathscr{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be the lifting of
f_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}F_{{\mathscr{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))}\,:=\,f_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,,\quad j=k\mod{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}\,.

Clearly,
F_{{\mathscr{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
is

{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-
periodic. The construction presented from
equation (
7.8
) to (
7.17
) provides a strictly
positive and
1-
periodic test function

J_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}=J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
of class
C^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
, provided that the smooth
functions
\Phi_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
defined below
(
7.17
) are chosen to enforce this
1-
periodicity
property. Thus,
J_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
can be considered as
defined on the torus.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
be
the probability measure given by the density:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\,:=\,\frac{\big[J_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}}{{\textsf{a}}(x)\,c_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\epsilon)}\,,

where

c_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\epsilon)
is a
normalization constant. Note that we exchanged the local potential

S_{{\mathfrak{D}}}
that appear in (
7.18
) by
S
.

By the proof of Lemma
7.6
, the sequence

\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
weakly converges to
\mu
. By the one
of Lemma
7.7
item (1),

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})\,\leq\,{\mathbb{I}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\omega)\,=\,{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)\,.

(8.1)

∎

We turn the condition (c). Up to the end of the section, we assume
that the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has infinitely
many closed irreducible classes which are all equivalent, and that the
Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is
irreducible. As observed above, in this case
S(1)
may be strictly
smaller than
S(0)
, see Figure
4
.

It follows from the condition (c) that there exists
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for
which

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)\}
is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-closed
irreducible class. Without loss of generality we may assume that

k=0
. As

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)\}
is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-closed
irreducible class, by translation-invariance of the jump rates,

R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}))\,=\,R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1))\,=\,0\,.

(8.2)

A similar identity holds for jump rates between

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(-1)

We start with a description of the energy landscape. We refer to
Figure
4
for an illustration. Recall the definition of the
maxima
\sigma_{k,k+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, given in
(
2.17
). Without loss of generality, we may assume that

\{0\}<\{\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}\}<{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)<{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)<\{1\}\,,\quad(0,\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})\cap{\mathcal{C}}=\varnothing\,.

That is,
\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
is the leftmost critical
point, to the right of
0
.

As

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)\}
is a
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
-closed
irreducible class, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}
, the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
,
0\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1

have all the same depth,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,,\quad 0\leq j\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1\,.

This equality and definitions (
2.18
), (
2.19
), (
2.22
)
imply that the barriers separating
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(1),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)
have all the same height,

S(\sigma)=S(\sigma^{\prime})\,,\quad\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{j=0}^{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-2}{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,.

By (
2.22
), (
8.2
), the barriers at the boundary of the closed
irreducible
class are higher:

S(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})\wedge S(\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-})>S(\sigma)\quad\text{for all}\;\;\sigma\in\bigcup_{j=0}^{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-2}{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,.

(8.3)

Assertion 8.2
.

Assume that case condition Rev(c) is in force. Then, there exist

\kappa>0
, and points

x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}<x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}
such that

{\textsf{b}}(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}),{\textsf{b}}(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})\neq 0
;

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}^{+}-1\,<\,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}\,<\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},0}^{-}\,<\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}^{+}\,<\,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}\,<\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},0}^{-}+1\,;

S(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-})=S(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+\kappa>S(x)\quad\text{for all}\;\;x\in(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})\,.

Proof.

By condition Rev(c), the lifted chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

has more than one closed irreducible class. Consequently, we may apply
the recursive procedure one more time to build a new layer at level

\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1
. The states of this new chain are the sets

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}-1})\}
and their translate. The assertion follows
by Lemma 4.6 in
[
27
]
.
∎

By translation invariance,

\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}=1+\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
, and
by definition,

\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}\leq\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
. Thus, by moving

\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
to
0+
we move

\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
(and therefore

\sigma_{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}
) to
1+
. Thus, by (
8.3
), and by
changing the value of
\kappa
if needed, we may assume
that

0\,<\,\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}\,<\,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}\,<\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},0}^{-}\,<\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}^{+}\,<\,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}\,<\,1\,.

(8.4)

\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
\sigma_{1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}+1
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(1)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(2)
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}
x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}
\kappa
\hat{\sigma}_{1}
\hat{\sigma}_{2}
\hat{m}
0
1

Figure 4.
This picture illustrates an example of a
non-reversible energy landscape as well as the periodic correction

\widehat{S}
for the case where
B>0
and the reduced chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is reversible. In this
example, the state space of the reduced chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
is composed by the three states

{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(1)
and

{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(2)
. The Markov chain cannot jump to the
right of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(2)
, nor to the left of

{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)
. The points

x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}
and the height
\kappa

are chosen according to Assertion
8.2
. Thus,

S(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-})-S({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))=S(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})-S({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+\kappa
. Finally, the behaviour of the reversible
potential
\widehat{S}
outside of the set

[x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}]
is represented in
brown.

The idea of the proof is simple and goes as follows. We define a

1-
periodic potential
\widehat{S}
that is equal to
S
on the set

(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})
, but has an extra
well that is deeper than all of the wells of
S
on

(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})
. See Figure

4
. Intuitively, this new potential induces a reversible
diffusion
X_{\epsilon}^{*}
on the torus having
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1

metastable levels and whose metastable behaviour on the interval

(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})
matches the one of

X_{\epsilon}
, up to level
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
. The state space

{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
of

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
can then be regarded as a
properly contained closed irreducible class of the reduced chain at
level
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
of
X_{\epsilon}^{*}
. The desired recovery
sequence will be the recovery sequence for
X_{\epsilon}^{*}

restricted to
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
, where

\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
is the set defined in (
7.12
).

Let
\widehat{S}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
1-
periodic
function of class
C^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

\widehat{S}(x)=S(x)
, for all
x\in(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})
;

(2)

on the interval
(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}-1,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-})
, the function
\widehat{S}
has exactly three critical points, denoted by
\hat{\sigma}_{1},\hat{m}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{2}
, each of them being nondegenerate, and satisfying

0\,<\,\hat{\sigma}_{1}\,<\,\hat{m}\,<\,\hat{\sigma}_{2}\,<\,x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}\,<\,1\,;

(8.5)

(3)

\widehat{S}(\hat{m})\leq S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))
;

(4)

\widehat{S}(\hat{\sigma}_{1})=\widehat{S}(\hat{\sigma}_{2})
and
\widehat{S}(\hat{\sigma}_{1})>S(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-})
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{-}}:=\hat{\sigma}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{+}}:=\hat{\sigma}_{2}
; and denote by
\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\subset(0,1)
be the set given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}}:=\bigcup_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}{\mathscr{W}}_{k-1,k}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\cup\{\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{-},\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{+}\}\,.

Let
\delta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}
,
\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}
, be the scales defined as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\delta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}}:=\sqrt{\frac{2\epsilon\log\epsilon^{-1}}{-\widehat{S}^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm})}}\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}}:=\frac{\epsilon}{\delta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}}\,,

and let
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
be the
{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-
neighborhood given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}}:=(\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{-}-\delta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{-}-\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{-}\,,\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{+}+\delta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{+}+\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{+})\,.

Let
J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
be the test function constructed via the exact same procedure introduced in the previous subsection, but changing
S
by
\widehat{S}
. By construction,
J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
is a function of class
C^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
whose support in contained in the set
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
. By (
8.5
), this set is properly contained in the interval
(0,1)
. Thus,
J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
can be understood as defined on the torus.

Let
\mu_{\epsilon}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
be the probability measure given by the density

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m_{\epsilon}(x)}:=\frac{[J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}(x)]^{2}e^{\widehat{S}(x)/\epsilon}}{{\textsf{a}}(x)c(\epsilon)}\,,

(8.6)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c(\epsilon)}
is a normalization constant that turns
m_{\epsilon}
into the density of a probability measure. The same proof of Lemma
7.6
yields that
\mu_{\epsilon}
weakly converges to
\mu
, provided that we set
l_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
and
k_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=0
. To conclude, it remains to show that

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\leq{\mathbb{I}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\omega)\,.

The proof is similar to the one of item (1) in Lemma
7.7
, provided that we replace the statement in Assertion
7.9
by the following

Proposition 8.3
.

Let
\mu_{\epsilon}
be the sequence of measures defined in (
8.6
). Then,

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\,\leq\,\sum_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\int_{B_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\cup\,\Lambda_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;\frac{e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}}{c(\epsilon)}\;dx\,+\,R(\epsilon)\,,

(8.7)

where
R(\epsilon)
is an error term that vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
.

Note that in the right-hand side of (
8.7
) we removed the integrals over the neighborhoods associated to the local maxima
\sigma_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}^{\pm}
of
\widehat{S}
. Consequently,

\bigcup_{k=1}^{{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1}\bigcup_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{k-1,k}}B_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\cup\,\Lambda_{\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\subset(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})\,.

Since the functions
S
and
\widehat{S}
coincide on
(x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+})
, this explains why we replaced
\widehat{S}
by
S
on the right-hand side of (
8.3
). The remaining of the section focuses on proving this proposition.

Recall that the test function vanishes outside the set
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
. The following result states that the discrepancy among the potentials
S
and
\widehat{S}
on the set
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\setminus[x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}]
is asymptotically negligible.

Assertion 8.4
.

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}{\epsilon^{2}}\int_{\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\setminus[x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}]}\,\frac{e^{-\widehat{S}(x)/\epsilon}}{c(\epsilon)}\,dx\,=\,0
.

Proof.

By item (4) in the definition of
\widehat{S}
and (
8.4
),

\inf_{x\in\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}}\widehat{S}(x)=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))+{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+\kappa\,.

(8.8)

This estimate, the fact that the test function vanishes outside of the set
\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
and the proof of Assertion
7.5
imply that the normalization constant
c(\epsilon)
given in (
8.6
) satisfies the following asymptotic estimate

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}e^{S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))/\epsilon}\,c(\epsilon)=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,.

(8.9)

To conclude the proof, observe that (
8.8
) and (
8.9
) yield

\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}{\epsilon^{2}}\int_{\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\setminus[x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}]}\,\frac{e^{-\widehat{S}(x)/\epsilon}}{c(\epsilon)}\,dx\,\leq\,\frac{C}{\epsilon^{5/2}}e^{-\kappa/\epsilon}\,\to\,0\,,

as
\epsilon\to 0
, where
C>0
is a finite and positive constant which doesn’t depend on
\epsilon
.
∎

Given
\delta>0
, let
\mu_{\epsilon}^{\delta}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
be the probability measure given by the following density

m_{\epsilon}^{\delta}(x)\,:=\,\frac{m_{\epsilon}(x)+\delta}{1+\delta}\,.

By Assertion
7.8
and the first part of the proof of Assertion
7.9
, it follows that

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\leq\liminf_{\delta\to 0}\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}{4\epsilon}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}{\textsf{a}}\,\Big\{\epsilon\Big[\frac{(m_{\epsilon}^{\delta})^{\prime}}{m_{\epsilon}^{\delta}}\,+\,\frac{{\textsf{a}}^{\prime}}{{\textsf{a}}}\Big]\,-\,\frac{{\textsf{b}}}{{\textsf{a}}}\Big\}^{2}d\mu_{\epsilon}^{\delta}\,.

By (
7.5
) in the definition of the test function,
\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}}|J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}^{\prime}(x)|=O(\epsilon^{-1/2})
. Then, differentiating
m_{\epsilon}^{\delta}
, expanding the square, applying the dominated convergence theorem and using the definition of
\widehat{S}
yields

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\,\leq\,\epsilon\,\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,\int_{x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}}^{x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}}\big[J^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}(x)\big]^{2}\;\frac{e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}}{c(\epsilon)}\;dx\,+\,C\,\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}{\epsilon^{2}}\int_{\Omega_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\setminus[x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-},x_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}]}\,\frac{e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}}{c(\epsilon)}\;dx,

for some finite and positive constant
C>0
that doesn’t depend on
\epsilon
. To conclude the proof of Proposition
8.3
, it remains to apply Assertion
8.4
and the definition of
J_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\epsilon}
to the right-hand side of the previous displayed inequality.

9.
The
\Gamma-\limsup
in the last time-scale: the
non-reversible case

In this section, we prove the
\Gamma-\limsup
in the remaining case.
Once this has been done, at the end of the section, we prove Proposition
2.4
and Theorem

2.7
.

In this section, we assume that in the last time-scale the reduced
Markov chain is non-reversible. Since we supposed that
B\geq 0
, in
view of Claims 2.A-2.C in
[
27
]
, this corresponds to the case
where

\begin{gathered}B>0\,,\quad R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))>0\quad\text{for all $k\in{\mathbb{Z}}$}\,,\\
\text{
$R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j-1))=0$ for some
$j\in{\mathbb{Z}}$}\,.\end{gathered}

(NonRev)

The next lemma asserts that in the layer
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
condition
(Rev) or condition (Nonrev) hold. In particular, Section
8

and
9
cover all cases of the last time scale.

Lemma 9.1
.

The Markov chains
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
,
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
satisfy one of the four conditions (Rev),
(Nonrev). Moreover,
{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
if conditions (Rev)(b)
or (Nonrev) are in force, and
{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1
, otherwise.

Proof.

By postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{7}
, the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
jumps only to nearest-neighbor sites,
and its jump rates are periodic in the sense of postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{9}
. By definition of the last layer
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
, the projected
chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has only one
irreducible class.

Denote by
\{j,\dots,k\}
, the unique

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
-irreducible class. We may
assume that
\{j,\dots,k\}
is contained in

\{0,\dots,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1\}
.

First, suppose that these two sets coincide:

\{j,\dots,k\}=\{0,\dots,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1\}
. Being a

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
closed irreducible set, it
is also a
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
closed irreducible set.
Thus, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}
, the sets

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),\dots,{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)
are at the same depth. By definition of
B
,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}))+B
.

Suppose that
B>0
. In this case, by postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}
, the Markov
chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
does not jump from

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)
. By
periodicity, it also does not jump from
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)

to
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(-1)
. If it jumps from

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
, condition
(Nonrev) is fulfilled. Moreover, the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has no closed irreducible class so
that
{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=0
and
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}={\mathfrak{q}}
.

It the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
does not jump,
condition (Rev)(c) is fulfilled. The Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has infinitely many closed
irreducible classes which are all equivalent. In this case,

{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
and
{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1
.

Suppose that
B=0
, so that

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}))
. By postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{7}
, the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
jumps
from
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
to

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)
if, and only if,
it jumps from
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)

to
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
. Suppose it
jumps. In this case, the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

is irreducible, condition (Rev)(b) is fulfilled, and

{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
.

If it does not jump, the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has infinitely many closed
irreducible classes which are all equivalent. Thus, (Rev)(c) holds,

{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
and
{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1
.

Assume now that the unique

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
-closed irreducible class

\{j,\dots,k\}
is a proper subset of

\{0,\dots,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1\}
. In this case, condition
(Rev)(a) is fulfilled,
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has infinitely many closed
irreducible classes which are all equivalent,

{\mathfrak{n}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
and
{\mathfrak{q}}=\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}+1
.
∎

The main result of this section reads as follows.

Proposition 9.2
.

Under the hypothesis
(NonRev)
, the functional
{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}

is a
\Gamma-\limsup
of
\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}
.

The proof of the
\Gamma-\limsup
for this non-reversible dynamics
follows the strategy proposed in
[
20
]
for finite-state Markov
chains and can be applied to other models. Fix a measure

\mu\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
. If
{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)=+\infty
, then the
bound

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\epsilon})\leq{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)

holds trivially for any sequence
\mu_{\epsilon}
that converges weakly
to
\mu
. Thus, by the definition of
{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\cdot)
, we may
assume that the measure
\mu
is given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mu(\cdot)}=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)\,,

(9.1)

for some probability measure
\omega\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
. The
measures
\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
appearing in this
formula were introduced in (
2.25
). By Lemma B5 in
[
20
]
, we
can assume that
\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))>0
for every

k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.

Fix a strictly positive measure
\omega\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
.
Let
\widehat{h}\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be a
strictly positive function that satisfies (
5.4
) with

l_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1
and
{\mathfrak{D}}_{1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. This function is
unique up to a multiplicative constant. Denote by

{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\widehat{h}}
the tilted generator given by
(
5.5
). By (
5.6
),
\omega

is the stationary state of the Markov chain with generator

{\mathbb{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\widehat{h}}
.

Denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}\colon{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{R}}}
,

k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, the smooth functions satisfying
(
5.9
). Fix
\lambda>0
, and denote by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}\colon{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{R}}}
the unique
solution of the resolvent equation

(\lambda-\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon})\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\,[\,(\lambda-\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})\,\widehat{h}\,]({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}\,.

(9.2)

The function
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
is clearly smooth. As

\widehat{h}
is strictly positive, by Lemma

5.4
, there exists
c_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0

such that

\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,\geq\,c_{0}\quad\text{for all}\;\;x\in{\mathbb{T}}\,,\;\;0<\epsilon<\epsilon_{0}\,.

Denote by
{\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
the generator tilted by
\phi_{{\mathfrak{q}},\epsilon}
, which already appeared just before the statement of
Lemma
3.1
,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}({\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}F)(x)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\,\Big\{[{\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon}(F\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})](x)-F(x)\,({\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})(x)\,\big\}

\displaystyle=\,\Big\{{\mathtt{b}}(x)\,+\,2\,\epsilon\,{\mathtt{a}}(x)\,\frac{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}^{\prime}(x)}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\Big\}F^{\prime}(x)\,+\,\epsilon\,{\mathtt{a}}(x)\,F^{\prime\prime}(x)\,,

and by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathbb{T}})
the
unique stationary state of
{\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
. By equation
(
9.4
) below, the density of
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
is
a strictly positive function of class
C^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
. Consequently, by
Lemma
3.2
,

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})=-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}{\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}}\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}\,.

In the remaining of the section, we show that
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}

is a recovery sequence for
\mu
:

Proposition 9.3
.

The probability measure
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
converges weakly to the
measure
\mu
.

Proposition 9.4
.

\limsup_{\epsilon\to 0}\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,{\mathscr{I}}_{\epsilon}(\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})\leq{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\mu)
.

The argument is organized as follows. First, we present an explicit
expression for the measure
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
. Then, we briefly
describe the main features of the energy landscape needed to estimate
the asymptotics of
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
. We complete the section
proving Propositions
9.3
and
9.4
, as
well as Theorem
2.7
.

The invariant measure of
{\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}

In
[
10
]
, the stationary measure of the process

X_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
, for
{\mathtt{a}}\equiv 1
, has been explicitly
computed. A simple adaptation gives the invariant measure for the
general situation. Let
\pi_{\epsilon}
,

m_{\epsilon}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m_{\epsilon}(x)}\;=\;\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\int_{x}^{x+1}e^{[S(y)-S(x)]/\epsilon}\,dy\;,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{\epsilon}(x)}\;=\;\frac{1}{c(\epsilon)}\,m_{\epsilon}(x)\;,

(9.3)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}c(\epsilon)}:=\int_{[0,1]}m_{\epsilon}(x)\,dx
.
As
m_{\epsilon}
is
1
-periodic, it can be considered as defined on

{\mathbb{T}}
, and
\pi_{\epsilon}
as the density of a probability measure on

{\mathbb{T}}
. A simple computation shows that this measure
is the stationary state of the diffusion (
2.7
). Similarly, let

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}\colon{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,}:=\,\frac{1}{Z_{\epsilon}}\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\int_{x}^{x+1}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(x)]/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,.

(9.4)

As
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
,
m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
is a strictly
positive function of class
C^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
. Integrating by parts gives
that

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}({\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}f)(x)\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,dx\,=\,0\,,

for every
f\in C^{2}({\mathbb{T}})
. This proves the next claim. Recall that

\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
represents the stationary state of the
diffusion induced by the generator
{\mathscr{L}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
.

Assertion 9.5
.

It holds that

\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(dx)=m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,dx
.

0
1
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(1)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(2)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(3)
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(4)
\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
\sigma_{0,1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}
\sigma_{0,1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
\sigma_{1,2}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
\sigma_{2,3}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
\sigma_{3,4}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}
\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\pm}+1

Figure 5.
Here we illustrate an example of an energy landscape of a
non-reversible diffusion whose reduced Markov chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
, at the last level of the
recursive construction, is irreducible and non-reversible. The depth
of the landscape, denoted by
{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, is represented
in purple. The state space of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is
composed by five states:

{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=\{{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0),\dots,{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(4)\}
. Among them,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)

and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(2)
are the only ones from which

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
cannot jump to the left. In
this example,
{\mathfrak{I}}_{0}=\{0,1\}
,
{\mathfrak{I}}_{1}=\{1\}
,

{\mathfrak{I}}_{2}=\{2,3,4\}
,
{\mathfrak{I}}_{3}=\{3,4\}
and
{\mathfrak{I}}_{4}=\{4\}
.

The energy landscape at level
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}

In this subsection we present some properties of the energy landscape
for the diffusion
X_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
in the last time-scale

\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. We refer to Figure
5
for an
illustration of the notation introduced below.

Recall that
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(i)
,
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, represent the lifting of
the sets
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
,
j\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, to
{\mathbb{R}}
:

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)<{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1)\,,\quad\Pi({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,=\,\Pi({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(i))\;\;\text{if}\;\;j\equiv i\;\;\text{mod}\;{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,.

We may assume, without loss of generality, that

\{0\}<\{\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}\}<{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0)<{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1)<\{1\}\,,

where
\sigma_{0,-1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
is given by (
2.17
).

We start with three simple observations. Recall from (
2.16
) the
definition of the set of absolute maxima
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
,

j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. As the process may always jump to the right, by
postulate
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})
, and the definition (
2.22
) of the jump
rates,

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))\,\leq\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\quad\text{and}\quad S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))=h^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}_{j}={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\quad\text{for all}\;\;j\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,.

(9.5)

As
B>0
,
S(x+1)<S(x)
so that

\sup_{y\geq x}S(y)\,=\,\sup_{y\in[x,x+1)}S(y)\quad\text{for all}\;\;x\in{\mathbb{R}}\,.

(9.6)

We claim that

S(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})=\sup_{x\geq\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}}S(x)\,.

(9.7)

Assume by contradiction that this is not the case. Then, we may choose

\hat{\sigma}\in(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+},\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}+1)

such that
S(\hat{\sigma})>S(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})
. Recall the
definition of
m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{\pm}(\hat{\sigma})
given below Lemma

5.3
. Let
0\leq k\leq{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1
be such that

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(\hat{\sigma})\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
. By (
9.5
),

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))\geq S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)))
. Then

{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\leq\,h_{0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}\,=\,S(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(0))\,<\,S(\hat{\sigma})-S(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\leq\,h_{k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}\,.

By (
2.22
), this implies that

R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}(k+1))=0
,
contradicting (NonRev). This proves the claim.

Recall from (
2.15
) the definition of the points
m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{\pm}
,

j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We claim that

\sup_{x\geq m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}S(x)\,=\,S(\sigma)\quad\text{for all}\;\;\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,,\;\;j\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,.

(9.8)

Fix
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. As,

\sigma\geq m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}
,

S(\sigma)\leq\sup_{x\geq m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}S(x)
.

Conversely, fix
x_{0}>m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}
. Recall from Lemma
5.4
the
definition of the points
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m^{\pm}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x_{0})}
. By definition,

m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x_{0})\geq x_{0}
, and

m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x_{0})\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
for some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. As

x_{0}>m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}
,
k>j
. Clearly,

x_{0}\in[m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k-1},m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}]
. Thus,

S(x_{0})\leq\,\sup\big\{S(y):m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k-1}\leq y\leq m^{-}_{{\mathfrak{q}},k}\,\big\}\,\vee\,\sup\big\{S(y):m^{-}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}\leq y\leq m^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}\,\big\}\,.

The first term on the right-hand side is
S({\mathscr{W}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{k-1,k})

by definition of this set. By Lemma
A.2
, the second term is also
bounded by
S({\mathscr{W}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{k-1,k})
, so that
S(x_{0})\leq S({\mathscr{W}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{k-1,k})
.

Therefore, as
k>j
, by both properties stated in (
9.5
),

S(x_{0})\leq S({\mathscr{W}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{k-1,k})={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k-1))\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))=S({\mathscr{W}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}_{j,j+1})=S(\sigma)\,.

This completes the proof of (
9.8
).

Fix
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}
be the largest
component to the right of
k
, which contains
k
and in which it is
always possible to jump to the left:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}:=\llbracket k\,,{\mathfrak{b}}_{k}\rrbracket\,\subset{\mathbb{Z}}

(9.9)

if
R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{k}+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{k}))=0
, and

R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j-1))>0
for all

k+1\leq j\leq{\mathfrak{b}}_{k}
. By definition, if

R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1),{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))=0
,
{\mathfrak{b}}_{k}=k

and
{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}=\{k\}
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
be
the set of minima belonging to the states with indices in

{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}
, and denote by
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})
its
lifting:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})}:=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})}:=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)\,.

By construction (which guarantees that the jump rates between
nearest-neighbor elements of
{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}
are positive), and postulates

{\mathcal{P}}_{8}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})
in Appendix
A
, all the local minima in

{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})
have the same depth:

S(m)=S(m^{\prime})\quad\text{for all}\;\;m\,,m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})\,.

(9.10)

As the jump rate to the right is always positive, by (
2.22
),

h_{k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
for all
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. Hence, by
(
9.10
),

S(\sigma)=S(\sigma^{\prime})\quad\text{for all}\;\;\sigma\,,\sigma^{\prime}\in\bigcup_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}{\mathscr{W}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}_{j,j+1}\,.

(9.11)

Let
{\textsf{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})\subset{\mathscr{M}}
be the set of minima that
are in the interval

(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+},\sigma_{{\mathfrak{b}}_{k},{\mathfrak{b}}_{k}+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-})
but do not
belong to the set
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\textsf{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})}:=\big[\,{\mathscr{M}}\cap(\sigma_{k-1,k}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+},\sigma_{{\mathfrak{b}}_{k},{\mathfrak{b}}_{k}+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-})\,\big]\setminus{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathfrak{I}}_{k})\;.

Denote by
V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
the quasi-potential given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)}:=S(x)\,-\,\sup_{y\in[x,+\infty)}S(y)\,.

By (
9.6
), the quasi-potential is well defined, and it is a

1-
periodic function. By (
9.7
),

V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\sigma_{-1,0}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+})=0
.

The next result follows directly by the definition of
{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}
,

k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, and (
9.8
). It relates the depth of the energy
landscape with the dynamics of the reduced Markov chain

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
.

Assertion 9.6
.

For every
j\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
,

V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(m)=V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})
. Denote this common value
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))}
. For every
j\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,

V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))-S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})\,=\,-\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,.

Proof.

Fix
j\in{\mathscr{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. By (
9.8
) and
the definition of
V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,

\displaystyle V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,=\,S(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,-\,S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})\,=\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,-\,S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})\,.

Assume that
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
has more than one element. Choose

m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)\setminus\{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}\}
. By postulate

{\mathcal{P}}_{5}
, (
2.20
) and (
9.8
),

S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})=\sup_{x\geq m}S(x)\,.

(9.12)

Consequently, since all of the minima in
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
have the
same depth, by the definition of
V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
and (
9.12
),

V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(m)=V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})
. To complete the proof, it
remains to recall the second identity in (
9.5
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
9.3

In this subsection, we establish Proposition
9.3
. The
proof relies on two lemmata which characterize the asymptotic behavior
of the measure
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
. The proofs are based on the
properties of the solution of the resolvent equation presented in
Subsection
5
.

We first show that the measure
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
is concentrated
on the neighborhoods of the local minima of order
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
, and that
the second integral in its definition can be carried over small
neighborhoods of the global maxima separating the minima.

Recall the definition of
r_{0}
, the height of the metastable wells,
given slightly above (
5.7
). By Lemma
A.2
, there exists

0<h_{0}<+\infty
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{0}}\,<\,\inf_{k\in\llbracket 0,{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1\rrbracket}S(\sigma_{k,k+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-})\,-\,S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}\,,\quad\text{where}\;\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S^{+}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}}\,:=\,\max\{S(x):x\in[m^{-}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k},\sigma^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathscr{W}}\}\,.

(9.13)

Fix
0<{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{0}}<r_{0}\wedge h_{0}
, and let

\eta_{0}>0
be a positive constant such that

0\leq\sup_{x\in(\sigma-\eta_{0},\sigma+\eta_{0})}S(\sigma)-S(x)<s_{0}\quad\text{for every $\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}$}\,.

As
s_{0}+r_{0}<2r_{0}<{\mathfrak{h}}_{1}
, and
s_{0}<h_{0}
,

\begin{gathered}(\sigma-\eta_{0}\,,\sigma+\eta_{0})\cap{\mathcal{E}}=\varnothing\quad\forall\,\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}\,,\;\;\text{so that}\;\;(\sigma-\eta_{0}\,,\sigma+\eta_{0})\cap{\mathscr{C}}=\{\sigma\}\,,\\
\text{and}\quad\inf_{x\in(\sigma-\eta_{0},\sigma+\eta_{0})}S(x)>S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}\;\;\text{for all}\;\;\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{k,k+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,,\;k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,.\end{gathered}

For each
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
x\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
, let

{\mathcal{W}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}
and
\widehat{{\mathcal{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)
be the sets
given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{W}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}}:=\bigcup_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\bigcup_{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}(\sigma-\eta_{0}\,,\sigma+\eta_{0})\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{{\mathcal{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)}=[x,x+1)\setminus{\mathcal{W}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}\,.

Assertion 9.7
.

There exists a positive constant
r_{1}>0
, independent of
\epsilon

such that

\displaystyle\inf_{x\in[0,1)\setminus\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)>-\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+r_{1}

\displaystyle\max_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\sup_{x\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\sup_{y\in\widehat{{\mathcal{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)}\big[\,S(y)-S(x)\,\big]<{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-r_{1}\,.

Proof.

Fix
x\in[0,1)\setminus\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k)
. Let
0\leq j\leq{\mathfrak{u}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-1
such that

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(x)\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
. Either

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(x)\leq x\leq\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}
or

\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}<x
. In the second case,

m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}(x)\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1)
and

\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}\leq x\leq m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{+}(x)
.

Assume that the first case is in force. The second one can be treated
similarly. Recall the definition of the set
{\mathtt{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
given
below Lemma
A.2
. Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M_{j}^{1}}:=\min_{m\in{\mathtt{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)}S(m)\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M_{j,j+1}^{2}}:=\inf\{S(z):z\in[\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-},\,\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}]\}\,,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M_{j}^{3}}:=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))+r_{0}\,,

where
r_{0}
has been introduced above (
5.7
). Denote by
M_{j}

the minimum over these quantities:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}M_{j}}:=\min\{M_{j}^{1},M_{j,j+1}^{2},M_{j}^{3}\}\,.

If
x\in[\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-},\,\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}]
, then

S(x)\geq M_{j,j+1}^{2}\geq M_{j}
, while if

x\in[m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(x),\,\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}]
, then

S(x)\,\geq\,\inf\Big\{S(y):y\in[m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(x),\,\sigma_{j,j+1}^{{\mathfrak{q}},-}]\setminus\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,\Big\}\,.

As
m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{-}(x)
belongs to
{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
, the infimum on the
right-hand side is attained either at an element of

{\mathtt{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
, or at the boundary of a set
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(m)
,

m\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
. The right-hand side is thus bounded below by

M_{j}^{1}\wedge M_{j,j+1}^{2}\geq M_{j}
. Thus,
S(x)\geq M_{j}
. On the
other hand, by Proposition
A.3
, Lemma
A.4
, and the
definition of the wells,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}s_{j}}:=M_{j}-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))>0
. In conclusion,

S(x)\geq M_{j}=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))+s_{j}\,.

(9.14)

Fix
y\geq x
. As
x\geq m^{-}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)\in{\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)
, by
(
9.12
),

S(y)-S(x)\leq\sup_{z\geq x}S(z)-S(x)\leq\sup_{z\geq m^{-}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)}S(z)-S(x)\leq S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1})-S(x)\,.

Recall from the second identity of Assertion
9.6
that

S({\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. Adding and
subtracting
S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))
, we can write the right-hand side of
the previous expression as

{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))-S(x)\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-s_{j}\,,

where the inequality follow by (
9.14
). Therefore,

-\,V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)=\sup_{y\geq x}S(y)-S(x)\leq{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-s_{j}\,.

This proves the first assertion with
r_{1}=\min_{j}s_{j}
.

We turn to the second one. Recall the definition of
\eta_{0}
given at
the beginning of this subsection. Fix
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
and

x\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
. By the proof of (
9.12
),

\sup_{y\in[x,x+1]}S(y)=\sup_{y\in[m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+},m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}+1]}S(y)\,.

Let
W_{k}
be the positive number given by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}W_{k}}:=\max\Big\{\,S(\sigma-\eta_{0}),\,S(\sigma+\eta_{0})\,:\sigma\in\bigcup_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\Big\}\,.

By (
9.11
), there exists a positive constant
s_{k}^{*}>0
such that

S({\mathscr{W}}_{k,k+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})-W_{k}>s_{k}^{*}
. By the definition
of the set
\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)
,

\sup_{y\in\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)}S(y)\,\leq\,W_{k}\,<\,S({\mathscr{W}}_{k,k+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})-s_{k}^{*}\,.

Subtracting
S(x)
in both sides of the previous expression, and
since, by the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
,

S(x)\geq S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))
,

\sup_{y\in\widehat{{\mathscr{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)}S(y)-S(x)<S({\mathscr{W}}_{k,k+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})-S(x)-s_{k}^{*}\leq S({\mathscr{W}}_{k,k+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})})-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))-s_{k}^{*}={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-s_{k}^{*}\,,

where the last identity follows from the second identity of Assertion

9.6
. This proves the second claim of the assertion with
r_{1}=\min\{s_{k}^{*}:k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\}
.
∎

Lemma 9.8
.

Fix a continuous function
f\in C({\mathbb{T}})
. Then, there exists a positive constant

{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}>0
, independent of
\epsilon
such that

\displaystyle e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\,\Big|\,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\int_{x}^{x+1}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(x)]/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(x)]/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx\,\Big|\,\leq\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}/\epsilon}\,.

Proof.

We need to show that
