e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\,\Big|\,\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\,\setminus\,\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\,\int_{x}^{x+1}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx\,\Big|\,\leq\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}/\epsilon}

and

e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\,\Big|\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\,\int_{\widehat{{\mathcal{W}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}^{+}(x)}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx\,\Big|\,\leq\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}/\epsilon}

for some
{\mathfrak{h}}^{*}>0
.

We consider only the first term, as the second one can be estimated in
the same way. The argument relies on Assertion

9.7
. By Lemmata

5.3
and
5.4
, and the
fact that
f
and
{\mathtt{a}}
are bounded functions, the left-hand side
of the penultimate displayed equation is bounded above by

\displaystyle C_{0}\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\,\int_{{\mathbb{T}}\,\setminus\,\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\int_{x}^{x+1}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy\,\,dx\leq C_{0}\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\sup_{x\in[0,1)\setminus\bigcup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}e^{-V_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(x)}\,.

for some finite and positive constant
C_{0}
, independent from

\epsilon
. By Assertion
9.7
, the
right-hand side of the previous expression is bounded by

C_{0}\,\exp\{-r_{1}/\epsilon\}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 9.9
.

For each
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))]/\epsilon}\,\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,=\,\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\,,

where
\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)
has been introduced in
(
2.21
).

Proof.

Fix
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}
. We start with a remark. By
the identity (
9.10
),

S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))=S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))
, and by Assertion

9.6
,

S(\sigma)={\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))
for all

\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
, so that

S(\sigma)\,=\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,+\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,=\,{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,+\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\quad\text{for all}\;\;\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}\,.

(9.15)

The proof is performed in four steps. Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H_{\epsilon}}:=\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,.

Step 1: Replacing
H_{\epsilon}
by
\widehat{H}_{\epsilon}

Fix
\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
, and recall the definition of
the minima
m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{\pm}
given in (
2.15
) and let

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}\colon[\sigma-\eta_{0},\sigma+\eta_{0}]\to{\mathbb{R}}
be the function defined by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(x)}:=\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})\big]+\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})\,{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\big]\,.

We claim that we can replace
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(\cdot)
by

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\cdot)
in the definition of

H_{\epsilon}
:

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))]/\epsilon}\,\big|\,H_{\epsilon}-\widehat{H}_{\epsilon}\,\big|\,=\,0\,.

(9.16)

where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{H}_{\epsilon}}:=\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,.

To prove (
9.16
), fix
\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. By the
definition of
\eta_{0}
,
\sigma
is the unique maxima in the interval

(\sigma-\eta_{0},\sigma+\eta_{0})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,\Big|\,e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy\,\leq\,e^{S(\sigma)/\epsilon}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,\Big|\,\,dy\,.

Therefore, by (
9.15
),

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))]/\epsilon}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\Big|\,\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,\Big|\,e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad\leq\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\Big|\,\frac{(\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)\,-\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y))\,(\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)+\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y))}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,\Big|\,dy\,.

By Lemmata
5.3
and
5.4
,
and since

{\mathbb{E}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+},m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\tau({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})]
, the previous expression is less
than or equal to

\displaystyle\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\big|\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)\,-\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y))\,\big|\,dy\,\leq\,\frac{C_{0}\,\eta_{0}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\tau({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}})/\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}]

for some finite constant
C_{0}
whose value changed from from the left
to the right-hand side. By Lemma
C.1
, the last expression
is bounded above by

C_{0}\,\eta_{0}\,\epsilon^{-1/2}\,\exp\{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}-{\mathfrak{h}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}-1}]/\epsilon\}
. This completes the proof of (
9.16
).

By (
9.15
) and (
9.16
), to prove the lemma it is enough to
show that

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\,\Big|\,\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,-\,\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\,\Big|=0\,.

(9.17)

Since the diffusion
X_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
is irreducible,

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(x)=\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})+{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,\big]\,\big[\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})\,-\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,\big]\,.

(9.18)

The argument is splited in two cases.

Step 2: Assume that

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))=\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))
.

Recall from (
2.21
) the definition of

\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)
. Fix
\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
.
Add and subtract
1/\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})^{2}
from

1/\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}
to estimate the expression appearing
on the left-hand side of (
9.17
) by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\,\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,\Big|\,\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}-\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})^{2}}\,\Big|\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\,\Big|\,\frac{1}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})^{2}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\,e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,dy\,-\,\frac{1}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))^{2}}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma)}}\,\Big|\,.

By the definition (
9.2
) of

\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(\cdot)
, Proposition

5.1
, and by Laplace’s method, the second
term vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
. By Lemmata

5.3
and
5.4
, the first
one is bounded by

\frac{C_{0}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\,\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,\big|\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,\big|\,dy

for some finite constant
C_{0}
. By (
9.18
), Proposition

5.1
, and since, by hypothesis,

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))=\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))
,
this expression vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
. This proves
(
9.17
) in the case where
\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j))=\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))
.

We turn to the case

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j+1))
. By (
9.18
) and Lemma
C.2
,

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{e^{S(x)/\epsilon}}{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}\,e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}\,\big[\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})\,-\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,\big]\,.

As

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))
,
by Proposition
5.1
,

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}^{\prime}
is bounded away from
0
. We may thus
write
\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}^{-2}
as

-[\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}^{\prime}]^{-1}[\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}^{-1}]^{\prime}
.
This identity together with (
9.15
) yields that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy

(9.19)

\displaystyle\quad=-\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\frac{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,dy}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\Big(\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(y)}\Big)^{\prime}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad=-\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\frac{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,dy}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\Big(\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma+\eta_{0})}\,-\,\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma-\eta_{0})}\Big)\,.

Step 3: Assume that

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))

and
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
is a singleton.

If
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
has exactly one maxima
\sigma
, by
(
9.15
) and the definition (
9.18
) of

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\cdot)
, the previous expression is equal to

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,\frac{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{[S(y)-S(\sigma)]/\epsilon}\,dy}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma+\eta_{0})\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma-\eta_{0})}\,\Big[\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\,\big]\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\sigma+\eta_{0}}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{{\mathfrak{q}},j}^{+}\,\big]\,\Big]\,.

By Laplace method, Lemma
C.2
, Proposition

5.1
; and the definition (
2.21
) of

\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)
, the previous expression is equal to

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{-S^{\prime\prime}(\sigma)}}\,\frac{1}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))}\,+R(\epsilon)=\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))}\,\,+\,R(\epsilon)\,,

(9.20)

where
R(\epsilon)
denotes an error that vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
.
This proves (
9.17
) when

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))
,
and
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
is a singleton.

Step 4: Assume that

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))

and
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
is not a singleton.

Recall from (
7.4
) that we denote by

\sigma^{1}_{j,j+1}<\dots<\sigma^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{j,j+1}}_{j,j+1}
the
elements of
{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. Re-ordering the terms in
the sum appearing in the last line of (
9.19
) yields that the first
line of (
9.19
) is equal to the sum
I_{1}(\epsilon)+I_{2}(\epsilon)
, where

I_{1}(\epsilon)\,=\,-\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\frac{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{[S(y-S(\sigma)])/\epsilon}\,dy}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})}\,\Big(\,\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},+}+\eta_{0})}\,-\,\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},-}-\eta_{0})}\,\Big)

and

\displaystyle I_{2}(\epsilon)\,=\,-\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\frac{\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{[S(y-S(\sigma)])/\epsilon}\,dy}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})}\,\sum_{r=1}^{{\mathfrak{r}}_{j,j+1}-1}\Big\{\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0})}-\frac{1}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}-\eta_{0})}\,\Big\}\,.

By (
9.20
),

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}I_{1}(\epsilon)\,=\,\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))}\,.

By Laplace’s method and Proposition
5.1
, the
factor multiplying the sum in
I_{2}(\epsilon)
is of order
1
since we
assumed that
\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\neq\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(j+1))
.

We turn to the remaining term. Fix
1\leq r\leq{\mathfrak{r}}_{j,j+1}-1
. By
the definition (
9.18
) of

\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle\big|\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0})^{-1}\,-\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}-\eta_{0})^{-1}\,\big|=\Big|\,\frac{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})-\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})}{\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0})\,\psi_{j,\sigma,\epsilon}(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}-\eta_{0})}\,\Big|\,A_{\epsilon}

where

\displaystyle A_{\epsilon}\,=\,\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0}}^{\epsilon}\Big(\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\Big)\,-\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\sigma_{j,j+1}^{{\mathfrak{q}},r+1}-\eta_{0}}^{\epsilon}\Big(\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+})\Big)\,\Big|\,.

By Lemmata
5.3
and
5.4
,
the first term on the right-hand side of the penultimate displayed
equation is bounded, uniformly in
\epsilon
. We turn to
A_{\epsilon}
.
By Lemma
C.2
,

\displaystyle A_{\epsilon}\,=\,\int_{\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0}}^{\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}-\eta_{0}}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy\,\Bigg/\,\int_{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+}}^{m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy\,.

Multiply the numerator and the denominator by

\epsilon^{-1/2}\,\exp\{-S(\sigma)/\epsilon\}
for some

\sigma\in{\mathscr{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. Laplace’s method yields that the
denominator converges to a positive constant. Since the torus has
length
1
, and
\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}
,

\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}
are consecutive global maxima in the
interval
[m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j}^{+},\,m_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},j+1}^{-}]
, the numerator is
bounded by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\exp\Big(\,\big[S(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r}+\eta_{0})\vee S(\sigma_{j,j+1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},r+1}-\eta_{0})\,-\,S(\sigma)\big]\,/\,\epsilon\,\Big)

As
\epsilon\to 0
, this expression vanishes, which concludes the
proof of the lemma.
∎

For each
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}H_{k,\epsilon}}:=\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,.

By the previous lemma,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))]/\epsilon}\,H_{k,\epsilon}\,=\,\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\,\cdot

(9.21)

Corollary 9.10
.

For each continuous function
f\in C({\mathbb{T}})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\epsilon}e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\sum_{\sigma\in{\mathcal{W}}_{j,j+1}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}}\int_{\sigma-\eta_{0}}^{\sigma+\eta_{0}}\frac{e^{[S(y)-S(x)]/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\pi_{1}(\{m\})\,f(m)

Proof.

Multiplying and dividing by
\exp\{-S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))/\epsilon\}

we can rewrite the left-hand side of the statement of the lemma as

\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{-[{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}+S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))]/\epsilon}\,H_{k,\epsilon}\,\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{[S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))-S(x)]/\epsilon}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\,dx\,.

(9.22)

For each
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, by Laplace’s method, the definition
(
9.2
) of
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(\cdot)
, and
Proposition
5.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{`e\to 0}\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\,e^{[S({\mathscr{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))-S(x)]/\epsilon}\,\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\,dx

\displaystyle\quad\,=\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\,\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(m)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(m)}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{S^{\prime\prime}(m)}}\,.

Thus, by (
9.21
), as
\epsilon\to 0
,
(
9.22
) converges to

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\Big(\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\Big)\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(m)}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{S^{\prime\prime}(m)}}\,f(m)\,.

To complete the proof of the corollary it remains to recall from
(
2.11
) the definition of the weights
\pi_{1}(\{m\})
.
∎

Proof of the main propositions

The proof of the next result is presented in Appendix
D
.

Lemma 9.11
.

There exists a constant
Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}>0
, such that, for every

k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}

\frac{1}{\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\,\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\,=\,Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,.

Proof of Proposition
9.3
.

By the definition of
Z_{\epsilon}
, Lemma
9.8
and
Corollary
9.10
, with
f=1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\epsilon}e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\,Z_{\epsilon}=\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\,.

By Lemma
9.11
, and since
\omega
is a
probability measure on
{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, this expression is equal to

Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.

Fix a continuous function
f\in C({\mathbb{T}})
. By (
9.4
),

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}f(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)=\frac{1}{Z_{\epsilon}}\,\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\int_{x}^{x+1}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx\,.

By the first part of the proof,

Z_{\epsilon}=[\,1+o(\epsilon)\,]\,Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\epsilon\,e^{{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}
. On the other hand, by Lemma

9.8
and Corollary
9.10
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,e^{-{\mathfrak{h}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}/\epsilon}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}f(x)\,\frac{1}{{\mathtt{a}}(x)}\,e^{-S(x)/\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)^{2}\int_{x}^{x+1}\frac{e^{S(y)/\epsilon}}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(y)^{2}}\,dy\,dx

\displaystyle\quad=\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\frac{\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)^{2})}{\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\frac{\pi_{1}(\{m\})}{\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(m)

By Lemma
9.11
, the definition
(
9.1
) of
\mu
, and (
2.25
), this expression
is equal to

\displaystyle Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}\frac{\pi(\{m\})}{\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}f(m)=Z_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\,\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}f(x)\,d\mu(x)\,.

The assertion of the proposition follows from the two previous
estimates.
∎

The first part of the previous proof characterizes the asymptotic
behavior of
Z_{\epsilon}
. This result together with Lemma

9.8
and the formula (
9.4
) for the
measure
\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}
yields that for any bounded function

g\colon{\mathbb{T}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,

\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}^{c}}g(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,=\,0\,.

(9.23)

where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}:=\cup_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
.

Proof of Proposition
9.4
.

By equation (
9.2
), adding and subtracting
\lambda

gives that

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{\theta_{\epsilon}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}({\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon}\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon})(x)}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,

\displaystyle=\,-\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\widehat{h})({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\,\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle+\,\lambda\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\frac{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}{\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,-\,\lambda\,,

where
\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}
,
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
, was defined in
(
5.9
). By Lemma
5.3
and (
9.23
), we may
restrict these integrals to
{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. By Proposition

5.1
, on the set
{\mathcal{E}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)
, the
function
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(\cdot)
is uniformly close to

\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))
. We may thus replace in the previous
integrals
\phi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)
by
\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))
at a cost which vanishes as
\epsilon\to 0
. After the
replacement, we may apply (
9.23
) to extend the integral to the
torus. In conclusion, the right-hand side is equal to

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\frac{(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\widehat{h})({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,+\,\lambda\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\epsilon}(x)\,-\,\lambda\,+\,o(\epsilon)\,.

By Proposition
9.3
, as
\epsilon\to 0
, this
expression converges to

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\frac{(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\widehat{h})({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu(x)\,+\,\lambda\,\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}(x)\,d\mu(x)\,-\,\lambda\,.

By definitions of the measure
\mu
and the function
\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}
,
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}}\zeta^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},k}\,d\mu=\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}(k))
. The previous expression is thus equal to

\displaystyle-\sum_{k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\frac{(\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\widehat{h})({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,.

By definition of
\widehat{h}
, it satisfies (
5.4
),
and the previous expression is equal to
{\mathbb{I}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\omega)
,
which completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.4
and Theorem
2.7

Recall the Definition
2.1
. By (
2.20
), condition
1 in the definition of
\Gamma-
expansion is in force. By Propositions

2.2
and
2.3
, condition 2 is in force for
p=-1
and

p=0
. By Propositions
6.2
,
7.1
,
8.1
,

9.2
, and Lemma
9.1
, condition 2 also holds for
1\leq p\leq\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}
. In particular, we have established Proposition
2.4
. Condition 3
follows by Propositions
2.2
,
2.3
,
6.1

and (
2.14
), (
2.26
). Condition 4 is part of the content of
Proposition
2.2
.

To complete the proof, it remains to show that condition 5 is in
force. This follows by (
2.24
) and the fact that

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
has only one closed irreducible
class. In fact, denote by
{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
the unique closed
irreducible class of
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
. Let

\omega^{*}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}})
be the probability measure
defined by

\omega^{*}({\mathcal{M}})\;:=\;\begin{cases}\nu^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}_{{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}({\mathcal{M}}),&\text{if }{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}},\\
0,&\text{ otherwise}\;,\end{cases}

(9.24)

where
\nu^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}_{{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}(\cdot)
is the
unique invariant measure of the process

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
restricted to

{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. Note that

\nu^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}_{{\mathfrak{R}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}=\omega^{*}
if

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)
is irreducible. Since,

{\mathbb{I}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}(\omega)=0
if, and only if,

\omega=\omega^{*}
; then,

\mu(\cdot)=\sum_{{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}\omega^{*}({\mathcal{M}})\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(\cdot)

is the unique element in the
0-
level set of

{\mathscr{J}}^{(\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}})}
. ∎

Appendix A
Metastable structure

In this section we present some of the properties of the hierarchical
metastable structure used in the article. The proofs of these properties
can be found in
[
27
, Appendix A]
.

At each step
q\geq 1
, the recursive procedure generates a layer

\Gamma_{q}=({\mathtt{P}}_{q},{\mathfrak{h}}_{q},{\mathbb{L}}_{q}),

where
{\mathtt{P}}_{q}:={\mathscr{S}}_{q}\cup\{{\mathscr{T}}_{q}\}
is a partition of the set
of
S-
local minima
{\mathscr{M}}
,
{\mathscr{S}}_{q}:=\{{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j):j\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
,

{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}>0
is an energy barrier, and
{\mathbb{L}}_{q}
is the generator of a

{\mathscr{S}}_{q}-
valued Markov chain, denoted by
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}(\cdot)
. If the
Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}
has two or more closed irreducible classes, we
may iterate the procedure. The recursion ends at the first step

{\mathfrak{q}}
at which the method yields a Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}}
with
only one or no closed irreducible classes.

In Section
2
we explicitly defined the energy barrier

{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}
and the reduced generator
{\mathbb{L}}_{q}
. Before stating the main
properties of the metastable structure, we give an explicit definition
of the partition
{\mathscr{S}}_{q}
for the case
q\geq 2
. We refer the reader
to Figure
6
for an illustration. Denote by

\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathfrak{R}}_{q-1}(k)
,
k\in K
, where
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}K
is a
subset of
{\mathbb{N}}
, the closed irreducible classes of the Markov chain

{\mathbb{X}}_{q-1}(\cdot)
. Let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}^{*}_{q}(k)}\,:=\,\bigcup_{i\colon{\mathscr{M}}_{q-1}(i)\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{R}}}_{q-1}(k)}{\mathscr{M}}_{q-1}(i)\;,\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{q}}\,:=\,\bigcup_{k\in K}{\mathscr{M}}^{*}_{q}(k)\;.

(A.1)

Hence,
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}
contains all the
S
-local minima
m
belonging to
some
{\mathbb{X}}_{q-1}
-recurrent state
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)
. In
[
27
, Section
2]
we showed that
K
is either empty, a singleton set or
countably infinite. In the later case, the family

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{q}^{*}(k)\}_{k\in K}
can be relabeled in such a way that
its members are ordered and the set
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}^{*}(0)
contains the
leftmost nonnegative minima in
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}
. Denote this new relabeled
family by
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The state space of
{\mathbb{X}}_{q}

is this relabeled family,

{\mathscr{S}}_{q}=\{{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)\}_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}.

We may now introduce the properties of the metastable structure. We
start with the ones related to each partition
{\mathscr{S}}_{q}
generated by
the recursion.

[
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}(q)
​​​

] For every
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)\neq\varnothing
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)\subset{\mathscr{M}}
.

Two subsets
C
,
D
of
{\mathbb{R}}
are said to be
well ordered
, if

C\cap D=\varnothing
and
x<y
or
x>y
for every
x\in C
and

y\in D
. In the first case this relation is represented by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C<D
, while in the second one it is denoted by
\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C>D
.

Definition A.1
.

Let
I
be a subset of
{\mathbb{Z}}
and consider a family
{\mathscr{F}}
of
subsets of
{\mathbb{R}}
indexed by
I

{\mathscr{F}}=\{D_{k}\}_{k\in I},\hskip 14.22636ptD_{k}\subset{\mathbb{R}},\hskip 4.2679pt\forall k\in I.

We say that
{\mathscr{F}}
is well ordered if
D_{k}<D_{l}
for every

k,l\in{\mathbb{I}}
with
k<l
.

[
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}(q)
​​​

]
{\mathscr{S}}_{q}
is well ordered according to
Definition
A.1
and its labeling is such that

m_{\char 17\relax_{q}}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(0)
, where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}j_{q}}\,:=\,\min\{k\geq 0:m_{k}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{q}\}\;.

(A.2)

Two subsets
A,B
of
{\mathbb{R}}
are said to be equivalent, a relation
represented by
A\sim B
, if

B=A+k,

(A.3)

for some
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. Here
A+k=\{x+k:x\in A\}
. Recall that
{\mathfrak{u}}_{q}\in{\mathbb{N}}
represents the number of elements in the state space
{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
of the projected chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{q}
. In other words,
{\mathfrak{u}}_{q}\in{\mathbb{N}}
is the number of (
A.3
)-equivalent classes of the set
{\mathscr{S}}_{q}
.

[
{\mathcal{P}}_{3}(q)
​​​

]
{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+{\mathfrak{u}}_{q})={\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)+1

for all
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. In particular,

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j+{\mathfrak{u}}_{q})\sim{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(j)
.

Let
C
be a nonempty subset of
{\mathbb{R}}
. We say that the elements in

C
have the same
depth
if
S(x)=S(y)
for all
x
,

y\in C
. For any set
A\subset{\mathbb{R}}
where all elements share the
same depth, let
S(A)
denote the common value of
S
at
A
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S(A)}\,:=\,S(x),\;\;\forall\;x\in A.

(A.4)

[
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}(q)
​​​

] For all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, the local minima in

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)
have the same
depth
.

We turn to the properties related to the energy barrier. Given two
subsets
A
,
B
of
{\mathbb{R}}
such that
A<B
, define the
barrier

between
A
and
B
as

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Lambda_{0}(A,B)}\,:=\,\sup_{x\in[x^{+}_{A},x^{-}_{B}]}S(x),

where
x^{+}_{A}
is the rightmost element of the closure of
A
, and

x^{-}_{B}
is the leftmost element on the closure of
B
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{+}_{A}}:=\sup\{x\in A\}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{-}_{B}}:=\inf\{x\in B\}
. For subsets
C
,
D
of

{\mathbb{R}}
such that
C<D
or
D<C
, let

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Lambda(C,D)}\,:=\,\Lambda_{0}(C,D)\;\;\text{if}\;\;C<D\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda(C,D)\,:=\,\Lambda_{0}(D,C)\;\;\text{if}\;\;D<C\;.

(A.5)

[
{\mathcal{P}}_{5}(q)
​​​

] For every
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for which

{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k)

).

[

] For every

,

\displaystyle\Lambda({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k),{\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k\pm 1))-S({\mathscr{M}}_{q}(k))\geq{\mathfrak{h}}_{q}.

Now, we turn to the properties associated to the Markov chains

Moreover, if

, then the
previous inequality is strict.

[

]
The jump rates are
{\mathfrak{u}}_{q}

Figure 6.
Here we represent an example of a metastable structure with 6
levels on
{\mathbb{R}}
, but 5 projected levels on
{\mathbb{T}}
. At level 1, the states
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k)
,
0\leq k\leq 6
,
are given by
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(k)=\{m_{k}\}
. These comprise all of the states contained in the interval
[0,1)
. The sets
{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(2)
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{1}(3)
belong to the same
{\mathbb{X}}_{1}(\cdot)-
recurrent
class, while the remaining states are absorbing. For
level 2,
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(0)=\{m_{0}\}
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(1)=\{m_{1}\}
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(2)=\{m_{2},m_{3}\}
, and
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(k)=\{m_{k+1}\}
, for

k\in\{3,4,5\}
. At this level,
{\mathscr{M}}_{2}(2)
is a

{\mathbb{X}}_{2}(\cdot)-
transient state, while the remaining states
are absorbing. The
states of level 3 contained in the interval
[0,1)
are

{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(0)=\{m_{0}\}
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(1)=\{m_{1}\}
,

{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(2)=\{m_{4}\}
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(3)=\{m_{5}\}
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(4)=\{m_{6}\}
. At this level,
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(0)
is

{\mathbb{X}}_{3}(\cdot)-
transient, the states
{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(1)
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(4)
are absorbing, and
\{{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(2),{\mathscr{M}}_{3}(3)\}
is a
closed irreducible class. Observe that, the projected chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{3}
can jump from
{\mathcal{M}}_{3}(0)=\Pi({\mathscr{M}}_{3}(0))
to
{\mathcal{M}}_{3}(4)=\Pi({\mathscr{M}}_{3}(4))
. Turning to level 4, we see the first relabeling (recall
property
{\mathcal{P}}_{2}
in Appendix
A
). Here
{\textsf{j}}_{4}=1
and we find the states

{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(0)=\{m_{1}\}
,
{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(1)=\{m_{4},m_{5}\}
and

{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(2)=\{m_{6}\}
. The state
{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(2)
is absorbing, while the set

\{{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(0),{\mathscr{M}}_{4}(1)\}
is a closed
irreducible class. At level 5,

{\mathscr{M}}_{5}(0)=\{m_{1},\;m_{4},\;m_{5}\}
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{5}(1)=\{m_{6}\}

compose the unique close irreducible class inside the interval
[0,1)
. By periodicity,
{\mathbb{X}}_{5}
has infinite closed irreducible classes and all of them are equivalent. Therefore,
{\mathfrak{n}}_{5}=1
and this is the last level for the projected metastable structure on
{\mathbb{T}}
,
\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}=5
, while there is one more level for the structure in
{\mathbb{R}}
,
{\mathfrak{q}}=6
. The state space of the Markov chain
{\mathbb{X}}_{6}
is
{\mathscr{S}}_{6}=\{{\mathscr{M}}_{6}(0)+k:k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
, the chain is transient and can only jump to the right.

Further properties of the metastable structure

Recall the definition of the set
{\mathscr{W}}^{(p)}_{k,k+1}
,

\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-}
,
\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,+}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, given
in (
2.16
) and (
2.17
), respectively.

The following result,
[
27
, Lemma A5]
, states that the energetic
barriers at
\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}
,
\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
are higher
than the ones in the interior of the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
. This result
implies that the diffusion
X_{\epsilon}(t)
, in time-scales of smaller
order than
\theta^{(p)}_{\epsilon}
, cannot escape the interval

(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}\,,\,\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})
.

Lemma A.2
.

For each
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\displaystyle S(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k})\,>\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S^{-}_{p,k}}\,:=\,\max\{\,S(x):x\in(\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},m^{+}_{p,k}]\cap{\mathscr{W}}\,\big\}\;,

\displaystyle S(\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\,>\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}S^{+}_{p,k}}\,:=\,\max\{S(x):x\in[m^{-}_{p,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1})\cap{\mathscr{W}}\}\;.

Let
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
be the set of local
minima of
S(\cdot)
that belong to the interval

[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]
, but are not contained in

{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
(see Figure
7
):

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)}\,:=\,\big(\,{\mathscr{M}}\cap[\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k},\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}]\,\big)\setminus{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\;.

\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}
\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}
m^{p,-}_{k}
m^{p,+}_{k}
\sigma^{p,+}_{k-1,k}

Figure 7.
Fix
p>1
and
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The state
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
is
represented by the cyan circles, while the set
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
is
composed by the green circles. As stated in Proposition
A.3
,
the local minima in
M_{p}(k)
have higher energy than the ones
contained in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
, which are all of the same height. With
respect to the escape barriers of the state
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
, we have only
one global maxima separating
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k-1)
, while
there are three between
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
. Here,

\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,+}=\sigma_{k-1,k}^{p,-}
, while

\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-}\,<\,\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,+}
. The orange circles
represent the local minima in between the left-most and right-most
maxima separating the states
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
. As
stated in Lemma
A.4
, these minima are higher than the ones
contained in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
.

The following proposition,
[
27
, Proposition A2]
, states that
every local minima in
{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)
is strictly
higher
than the
ones contained in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
. See Figure
7
.

Proposition A.3
.

For all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\min\{S(m):m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(k)\,\}\,>\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k))\;.

The next result,
[
27
, Lemma A11]
, states that the local minima in
between the leftmost and rightmost absolute maxima separating any two
states, say
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)
and
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
, are higher than the ones
belonging
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k)\cup{\mathscr{M}}_{p}(k+1)
. See Figure
7
. The
case
p=1
is excluded because

\sigma_{k,k+1}^{1,-}=\sigma_{k,k+1}^{1,+}
, for all
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Lemma A.4
.

Fix
2\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, and suppose that

\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}<\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}
. Then,

\min\big\{S(x):x\in[\sigma^{p,-}_{k,k+1}\,,\,\sigma^{p,+}_{k,k+1}]\,\big\}\,>\,\max\{S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k)),S({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1))\}\;.

We conclude this subsection with a result regarding the depth of the
local minima belonging to the same
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}-
equivalent class, for any
intermediate layer
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
. Fix a
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}-
equivalent class

{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{p}
. Recall the definition of the set

{\mathscr{W}}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{(p)}
given in (
7.3
), and the definitions
of the local maxima
\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{-},\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{+}
given in
(
7.11
). Let
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})\subset{\mathscr{M}}_{p}
be the set of local
minima of level
p
belonging to the equivalence class
{\mathfrak{D}}

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}:=\bigcup_{{\mathscr{M}}\in{\mathfrak{D}}}{\mathscr{M}}.

Note that, by properties
{\mathcal{P}}_{4}
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{8}
in the previous
subsection, all the local minima in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
have the same
depth,

S(m)=S(m^{\prime})\,,\quad m,m^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})\,.

(A.6)

Let
{\textsf{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
be set of local minima of
S(\cdot)
that belong
to the interval
[\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{-},\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{+}]
, but are not
contained in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\textsf{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}:=\big(\,{\mathscr{M}}\cap[\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{-},\sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{+}]\,\big)\setminus{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})\;.

The next result states that every local minima in
{\textsf{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
is
strictly higher than the ones in
{\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
.

Proposition A.5
.

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
. For every
{\mathbb{X}}_{p}(\cdot)-
equivalent
class
{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathscr{S}}_{p}
,

\min\{S(m):m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})\,\}\,>\,S({\mathscr{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}}))\;.

Proof.

Fix
m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})
. Then, either

m\in{\mathscr{M}}\cap[\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-},\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,+}]
or

m\in{\textsf{M}}_{p}(j)
, for some
k
,
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. In the first case, the
result follows by Lemma
A.4
and (
A.6
). In
the second case, it follows by Proposition
A.3
and
(
A.6
).
∎

Appendix B
Metastable states

In this section we introduce a useful formula for the metastable
states defined in (
2.25
). Recall that

S_{p}
denotes the index set of the state space
{\mathcal{S}}_{p}
,

1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
.
This formula seeks to relate the metastable states

\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}^{p+1}
,
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in S_{p+1}
, with
the stationary states of
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)
. This requires
some preparation. Fix
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{D}}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{p}

be a
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)-
equivalent class with two or more
elements. Recall from Section
5
the definition of the
generator
\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}
, introduced in (
5.1
), and
that we denote by
\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{p}\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathfrak{D}})
the stationary
state of the Markov chain induced by
\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}
.

Lemma B.1
.

Assume that the Markov chain
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)
is
reducible. Then, the generator
\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{p,{\mathfrak{D}}}
induces a
reversible process and the stationary state
\nu_{{\mathfrak{D}}}^{p}
is given by

\nu^{p}_{{\mathfrak{D}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(j))=\frac{1}{Z_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}\,\pi_{p}(j)\,,\quad{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(j)\in{\mathfrak{D}}\,,\quad\text{where}\;\;Z_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})\,:=\,\sum_{i\in S_{p}:{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(i)\in{\mathfrak{D}}}\pi_{p}(i)\,,

and
\pi_{p}(j)
is given by (
2.21
).

Proof.

Let
\nu^{*}
be the probability measure defined by

\nu^{*}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,:=\,\frac{1}{Z_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}\,\pi_{p}(k)\,,\quad{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k)\in{\mathfrak{D}}.

We claim that
\nu^{*}
satisfies the detailed balance conditions. Fix

k\in S_{p}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1)\in{\mathfrak{D}}
. Since

{\mathfrak{D}}
is an
\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}
-equivalent class, and

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)
is a reducible Markov chain jumping only to
nearest neighbor sets,
\widehat{R}_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1))>0
and

\widehat{R}_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))>0
. Thus, by (
2.22
),

h^{p,+}_{k}=h^{p,-}_{k+1}={\mathfrak{h}}_{p}
, and

\displaystyle\nu^{*}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1))=\frac{1}{Z_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}\,\pi_{p}(k)\,\frac{1}{\pi_{p}(k)\,\sigma_{p}(k,k+1)}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{Z_{p}({\mathfrak{D}})}\,\pi_{p}(k+1)\,\frac{1}{\pi_{p}(k+1)\,\sigma_{p}(k,k+1)}=\nu^{*}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1))\,\widehat{R}_{p}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k+1),{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(k))\,,

as claimed.
∎

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
and
k\in S_{p+1}
. Denote by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{{\mathfrak{R}}}_{k}^{p}}
the

\widehat{{\mathbb{X}}}_{p}(\cdot)-
closed irreducible class generating the
state
{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)
in the sense of (
A.1
). Let

\nu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}^{p}\in{\textsf{P}}(\widehat{{\mathfrak{R}}}_{k}^{p})
,

k\in S_{p+1}
, be the probability measure defined by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\nu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}^{p}({\mathcal{M}})}\,=\,\nu_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{R}}}_{k}^{p}}^{p}({\mathcal{M}})\,,\quad{\mathcal{M}}\in\widehat{{\mathfrak{R}}}_{k}^{p}.

(B.1)

The next result provides a recursive formula for the measures

\mu^{p}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r)}(\cdot)
,
0\leq r<{\mathfrak{u}}_{p}
, introduced in
(
2.25
).

Proposition B.2
.

Fix
1\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
and
k\in S_{p+1}
. Then,

\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}^{p+1}(\cdot)\,=\,\sum_{r\in S_{p}:{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r)\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}\nu^{p}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}({\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r))\,\mu^{p}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(r)}(\cdot)\,.

Proof.

The case
p=1
follows from the definition (
2.25
) of the measures

\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{2}(k)}^{2}(\cdot)
,
\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}(r)}^{1}(\cdot)
, and
Lemma
B.1
.

Fix
2\leq p<{\mathfrak{q}}
,
k\in S_{p+1}
. By (
2.25
) and (
A.1
),

\displaystyle\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}^{p+1}=\sum_{l\in S_{p}:{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l)\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}\frac{\pi_{p}(l)}{\pi_{p+1}(k)}\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l)}\frac{\pi_{1}(\{m\})}{\pi_{p}(l)}\,\delta_{m}=\sum_{l\in S_{p}:{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l)\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{p+1}(k)}\frac{\pi_{p}(l)}{\pi_{p+1}(k)}\,\mu_{{\mathcal{M}}_{p}(l)}^{p}\,.

It remains to apply Lemma
B.1
to complete the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Hitting times

In this section, we present some estimates of the

X_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
-hitting time of wells. The following result,

[
27
, Lemma 4.3]
, states that the set
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}
is reached in the
time-scale
\epsilon^{-1}e^{{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}/\epsilon}
.

Lemma C.1
.

There exists a finite constant
C_{0}=C_{0}({\mathfrak{q}},{\mathtt{a}}(\cdot))
such
that

\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x}^{\epsilon}[\,\tau({\mathcal{M}}_{p})\,]\,\leq\,C_{0}\,\epsilon^{-1}\,e^{{\mathfrak{h}}_{p-1}/\epsilon}

for all
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
, provided
{\mathfrak{h}}_{0}=0
.

Recall from the statement of Lemma
5.4
that
for each
x\in{\mathbb{T}}
, we denoted by
m_{p}^{-}(x)
, and
m_{p}^{+}(x)
, the
closest minima of level
p
to the left, and right of
x
,
respectively. Let
m_{p}^{\pm,*}(x)
be their lifted
versions in
{\mathbb{R}}
satisfying
\Pi(m_{p}^{\pm,*}(x))=m_{p}^{\pm}(x)
,

m_{p}^{-,*}(x)\in(0,1)
, and

m_{p}^{+,*}(x)\in(m_{p}^{-,*}(x),m_{p}^{-,*}(x)+1)
. By definition of

m_{p}^{\pm}(x)
, if we denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x^{*}}
the real number such that

x^{*}\in(0,1]
and
\Pi(x^{*})=x
,

m_{p}^{-,*}(x)<x^{*}<m_{p}^{+,*}(x)
.

The next result
provides an explicit expression for the probability that the diffusion

X_{\epsilon}
, conditioned on starting at some arbitrary point

x\in{\mathbb{T}}
, reaches
{\mathcal{M}}_{p}
by hitting
m_{p}^{-}(x)
first.

Lemma C.2
.

Fix
1\leq p\leq{\mathfrak{q}}
. Then, for every
x\in{\mathbb{T}}

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{p}^{-}(x))<\tau(m_{p}^{+}(x)\,\big]=\frac{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{x^{*}}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{m_{p}^{+,*}(x)}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}\,\cdot

In particular, for every
k\in S_{p}
,

x^{-}\in(m_{p,k}^{+},\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-})
, and

x^{+}\in(\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,+},m_{p,k+1}^{-})
, there exist positive
constant
C_{x^{\pm}}
,
h_{x^{\pm}}
which depend on
x^{\pm}
, but
not on
\epsilon
, such that

{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{-}}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{p,k+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{p,k}^{+})\,\big]\leq\frac{C_{x^{-}}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}e^{-h_{x^{-}}/\epsilon}\,,\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{+}}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{p,k}^{-})<\tau(m_{p,k+1}^{+})\,\big]\leq\frac{C_{x^{+}}}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}e^{-h_{x^{+}}/\epsilon}

for all
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

Fix
x\in{\mathbb{T}}
. For each
\epsilon>0
, let

\xi_{\epsilon}\colon[m_{p}^{-}(x),m_{p}^{+}(x)]
be the function given by

\xi_{\epsilon}(z)=\frac{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{z}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{m_{p}^{+,*}(x)}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}\,,\quad z\in[m_{p}^{-}(x),m_{p}^{+}(x)]\,.

By a direct computation, it can be verified that
\xi
solves the
following equation

\begin{cases}({\mathscr{L}}_{\epsilon}u)(z)=0\,,\quad\text{for}\ z\in(m_{p}^{-}(x),m_{p}^{+}(x))\\
u(m_{p}^{-}(x))=0\,,\quad u(m_{p}^{+}(x))=1\,.\end{cases}

Therefore, the process
\xi_{\epsilon}(X_{\epsilon}(t\wedge\tau))
,

\tau:=\tau(m_{p}^{-}(x),m_{p}^{+}(x))
, is a bounded martingale with
respect to
{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}
. By the optional stopping
theorem,

{\mathbb{P}}_{x}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{p}^{-}(x))<\tau(m_{p}^{+}(x))\,\big]=\xi_{\epsilon}(x)=\frac{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{x^{*}}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}{\int_{m_{p}^{-,*}(x)}^{m_{p}^{+,*}(x)}e^{S(y)/\epsilon}\,dy}\,.

We turn to the second statement. Fix
k\in S_{p}
and

x^{-}\in(m_{p,k}^{+},\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-})
. Let
h_{0}
be the maximum
of
S
in the interval
[m_{p,k}^{+},x]
. As
x<\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-}
,
h_{0}<S(\sigma_{k,k+1}^{p,-})=h^{p,+}_{k}
.
By the first assertion of the lemma, estimating the numerator by the
maximum value of the function multiplied by the length of the interval
(which is bounded by
1
), and Laplace’s method,

{\mathbb{P}}_{x^{-}}^{\epsilon}\big[\,\tau(m_{p,k+1}^{-})<\tau(m_{p,k}^{+})\,\big]\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\epsilon}}e^{[h_{0}-h^{p,+}_{k}]/\epsilon}\,,

for some positive constant
C>0
which doesn’t depend on

\epsilon
. This establishes the first estimate of the second
assertion of the lemma. The other one is proved similarly.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Lemma
9.11

The proof is carried out in three main steps. We first express the sum
on the left-hand side of Lemma
9.11
in terms of the jump rates of
the tilted chain.

Recall from Section
9
that
\omega\in{\textsf{P}}({\mathcal{S}}_{{\mathfrak{q}}})

is a strictly positive measure, and that

\widehat{h}\colon{\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
is a
strictly positive function, unique up to a multiplicative constant,
that satisfies (
5.4
) with
l_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}=1

and
{\mathfrak{D}}_{1}^{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. Recall,
furthermore, that

\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}},\widehat{h}}=:{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
represents the tilted generator given by
(
5.5
). Denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}}
the
associated nearest-neighbor jump rates, and write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k\pm 1)}:=\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k\pm 1))
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k\pm 1)}:=R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k),{\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k\pm 1))
, for

k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. By (
5.6
),
\omega

is the stationary state of the Markov chain with generator

\widehat{{\mathbb{L}}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
.

We claim that for all
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\,\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)\,\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))^{2}\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}{\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j))\widehat{h}({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j+1))}

(D.1)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\,\sum_{j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}}\frac{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)\cdots\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j-1)}{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\cdots\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j-1,j)}\,\frac{1}{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)}\,\cdot

Mind that for
j=k
the sum becomes
\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)^{-1}
.

The proof of this identity is elementary. Fix
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
. First
observe by (
2.22
) that

\frac{\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k)}{\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)}\,=\,\frac{R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)\cdots R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j-1)}{R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\cdots R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j-1,j)}\quad\text{for}\;\;j\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{k}\,,\;\;j\neq k\,.

To conclude, use (
2.22
) again to write that

\pi_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j)\,\sigma_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)=R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(j,j+1)^{-1}
, and
express the jump rates
R_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(i,i\pm 1)
in terms of the tilted
ones
\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(i,i\pm 1)
.

As
\omega(\cdot)
is a stationary state, and the Markov chain jumps
only to nearest-neighbor sites, for every
k\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,

\displaystyle\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k-1))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k-1,k)\,+\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)

\displaystyle\quad\,=\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k-1)\,+\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\,.

Therefore,

Z\,:=\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\,-\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)\quad\text{is constant}\,.

(D.2)

If
Z
were equal to
0
the process would be reversible, which, by
the hypothesis (NonRev), is not the case. As the Markov chain may not
jump to the left at certain sites,
Z>0
.

We claim that the sum in (
D.1
) is equal to
1/Z
. Denote it by

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathtt{F}}(k)}
. The proof is by induction. Fix
j\in S_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}
,
and recall from (
9.9
) the definition of the set
{\mathfrak{I}}_{j}
, and
that
{\mathfrak{b}}_{j}
represents the right-most element of
{\mathfrak{I}}_{j}
.

By inspection,
{\mathtt{F}}({\mathfrak{b}}_{j})=1/Z
. Assume that
{\mathtt{F}}(i)=1/Z

for
k+1\leq i\leq{\mathfrak{b}}_{j}
and that
k\in{\mathfrak{I}}_{j}
. By the definition
of
{\mathtt{F}}(\cdot)
,

{\mathtt{F}}(k)\,=\,\frac{1}{\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))}\,\Big\{\,\frac{1}{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)}\,+\,\frac{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)}{\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)}\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))\,{\mathtt{F}}(k+1)\,\Big\}\,.

Thus
{\mathtt{F}}(k)=1/Z
if, and only if,

\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\,(1/Z)\,=\,\Big\{\,1\,+\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))\,{\mathtt{F}}(k+1)\,\Big\}\,.

By the induction hypothesis,
{\mathtt{F}}(k+1)=1/Z
. We may thus replace

{\mathtt{F}}(k+1)
by

[\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k,k+1)\,-\,\omega({\mathcal{M}}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1))\,\widehat{R}_{\widehat{{\mathfrak{q}}}}(k+1,k)]^{-1}
.
After this replacement a straightforward calculation shows that the
previous identity holds. This completes the proof of the Lemma

9.11
.
